By
Step 1
and
Step 2
, we observe that the full-subquiver consisting of the
rightmost 6-vertices in (
8.2
) are isomorphic to the
rightmost 6-vertices of
{}^{s_{i}\xi}{\widetilde{\triangle}}
.
Furthermore, since the local circumstance of
(i,\xi_{i}-4)
in
(\mu_{(i,\xi_{i}-2)}\circ\mu_{(i,\xi_{i})})({{}^{\xi}{\widetilde{\triangle}}})

is the same as the one of
(i,\xi_{i}-2)
in
\mu_{(i,\xi_{i})}({{}^{\xi}{\widetilde{\triangle}}})
, we can apply an induction on
k
for the valued quiver

\displaystyle(\mu_{(i,\xi_{i}-2k)}\cdots\circ\mu_{(i,\xi_{i}-4)}\circ\mu_{(i,\xi_{i}-2)}\circ\mu_{(i,\xi_{i})})({{}^{\xi}{\widetilde{\triangle}}})\qquad\text{ for all }k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 1}.

Finally, our assertion comes from the definition of
{}^{s_{i}\xi}{\widetilde{\triangle}}
.
∎

The following proposition is a direct consequence of Proposition
8.2
and the definition of

{{}^{\xi}{\widetilde{\triangle}}}
.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
i,j
be sources of
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
. Then we have

\displaystyle({}^{\textbf{{j}}}_{s_{i}\xi}\upmu\circ{}^{\textbf{{i}}}_{\xi}\upmu)({}^{\xi}{\widetilde{\triangle}})\simeq({}^{\textbf{{i}}}_{s_{j}\xi}\upmu\circ{}^{\textbf{{j}}}_{\xi}\upmu)({}^{\xi}{\widetilde{\triangle}}).

Thus, for any
Q
-adapted reduced expression
s_{i_{1}}\cdots s_{i_{n}}
of the Coxeter element
\tau_{Q}
,

(8.10)

\displaystyle{}^{\textbf{{Q}}}\upmu\mathbin{:=}{}^{\textbf{{i}}_{n}}_{s_{i_{n}}\cdots s_{i_{1}}\xi}\upmu\circ\cdots\circ{}^{\textbf{{i}}_{2}}_{s_{i_{1}}\xi}\upmu\circ{}^{\textbf{{i}}_{1}}_{\xi}\upmu\text{ is well-defined.}

Theorem 8.4
.

For Dynkin quivers
Q=(\triangle,\xi)
and
Q=(\triangle,\xi^{\prime})
, there exists a sequence of mutations
\upmu
such that

\displaystyle\upmu({{}^{\xi}{\widetilde{\triangle}}})\simeq{}^{\xi^{\prime}}{\widetilde{\triangle}}\qquad\text{as valued quivers}.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle{}^{\textbf{{Q}}}\upmu({}^{\xi}{\widetilde{\triangle}})\simeq{}^{\xi}{\widetilde{\triangle}}\qquad\text{as valued quivers}.

Proof.

This assertion follows from (
2.6
), (
2.8
) and Proposition
8.2
.
∎

8.3.
Quantum cluster algebra structure on
\mathfrak{K}_{q,\xi}(\mathfrak{g})

For each
s\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
, we denote by
{}^{(s)}\xi
the height function such that
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{i}\in\{s,s-1\}
for all
i\in{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}_{0}
and
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108ptQ=({\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt},{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi)
.
For a height function
\xi
, we set

\displaystyle{}^{\xi}\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})\mathbin{:=}\mathop{\mbox{\normalsize$\bigoplus$}}\limits_{m\in{{}^{\xi}}\hskip-0.60275pt\mathcal{M}_{+}}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}](F_{q}(\underline{m}))_{\leqslant\xi}\subset{}^{\xi}\mathcal{X}_{q}.

Note that
{}^{\xi}\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})=(\cdot)_{\leqslant\xi}\left(\mathfrak{K}_{q,\xi}(\mathfrak{g})\right)\simeq\mathfrak{K}_{q,\xi}(\mathfrak{g})
.

For simplicity of notations, we set

(1)

{}^{s}{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}\mathbin{:=}{}^{{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi}{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}
,
{}^{s}\mathcal{X}_{q}\mathbin{:=}{}^{{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi}\mathcal{X}_{q}
,
(-)_{\leqslant s}\mathbin{:=}(-)_{\leqslant{}^{(s)}\xi}
,

(2)

{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptL\mathbin{:=}{}^{{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi}L
,
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptB\mathbin{:=}{}^{{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi}\widetilde{B}
,

(3)

{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\upmu\mathbin{:=}{}^{{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\textbf{{Q}}}\upmu
,
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})\mathbin{:=}{}^{{}^{(s)}\xi}\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})

and
\mathfrak{K}_{q,s}(\mathfrak{g})\mathbin{:=}\mathfrak{K}_{q,{}^{(s)}\xi}(\mathfrak{g}).

From now on,
we fix
s\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
and
{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}
.
Let us denote by
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{A}_{q}
the quantum cluster algebra whose initial seed is

(8.11)

\displaystyle{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{S}\mathbin{:=}(\{v_{i,p}\mathbin{:=}\underline{m^{(i)}[p,s]}\}_{(i,p)\in{}^{s}\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-2.77277pt\raisebox{0.54247pt}{{\scalebox{.38}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}\hskip 2.84526pt}}_{0}},{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptL,{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptB).

For
n\geqslant 0
, let
v_{i,p}^{(n)}
be the quantum cluster variable obtained at vertex
(i,p)
after applying the sequence of mutations
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\upmu

n
-times.
Then we give a quantum cluster algebra algorithm to compute
F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b])
for KR-monomials
m^{(i)}[a,b]
.

Proposition 8.5
.

(cf.
[
30
, Theorem 3.1]
,
[
5
, Proposition 6.3.1]
)
For each
(i,p)\in{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
and
n\geqslant 0
,

(8.12)

\displaystyle v_{i,p}^{(n)}={{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[p-2n,s-2n])\mathbin{:=}(F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[p-2n,s-2n]))_{\leqslant s}.

In particular, if
2n\geqslant\mathsf{h}
, we have

v_{i,p}^{(n)}=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[p-2n,s-2n]).

Proof.

Let us apply induction on
n
for this assertion. For
n=0
, it follows from (
5.25
) in Proposition
5.25
.
Let
n\geqslant 0
and
(i,p)\in{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
. Suppose we have applied
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\upmu

n
-times on
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{S}
, and
(n+1)
-times
on all vertices preceding
(i,p)
in the sequence
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\upmu
, and that all those previous vertices satisfy (
8.12
).

Thanks to Theorem
8.4
, the corresponding valued quivers coincide up to a shift of spectral parameters in labeling of vertices.
Then, the argument in the proof of Proposition
8.2
tells us that the vertex
(i,p)
is vertically sink or horizontally source, that is, one of the following configurations:

\textstyle{(j,p+{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{j,i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{j,i},\mathsf{c}_{i,j}\lrcorner}
\textstyle{(i,p-2)}
\textstyle{(i,p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(i,p+2)}
\textstyle{(j^{\prime},p+{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{j^{\prime}i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{j^{\prime}i},\mathsf{c}_{ij^{\prime}}\lrcorner}

\textstyle{(j,p+{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{j,i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{j,i},\mathsf{c}_{i,j}\lrcorner}
\textstyle{(i,p-2)}
\textstyle{(i,p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(i,p+2)}

\textstyle{(i,p-2)}
\textstyle{(i,p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(i,p+2)}
\textstyle{(j^{\prime},p+{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{j^{\prime}i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{j^{\prime}i},\mathsf{c}_{ij^{\prime}}\lrcorner}

where
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{k,i}\mathbin{:=}(-1)^{\delta\bigl({{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi_{k}<{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi_{i}\bigr)}
for
k\in{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}_{0}
with
d(i,k)=1
.
In this proof, we only consider the first one in (
8.3
) since the computation below is almost identical for the other cases.

By the definition of
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi
, we have

{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{j,i}={{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{j^{\prime},i}
for all
j,j^{\prime}\in{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}_{0}
with
d(j,i)=d(j^{\prime},i)=1
.
Now let us assume that
i
is a source of
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi
since the proof for the cases when
i
is a sink of
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi
is similar.
Then the quantum exchange relation has the form

(8.31)

\begin{split}v_{i,p}^{(n+1)}*v_{i,p}^{(n)}=q^{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}v_{i,p+2}^{(n+1)}\cdot v_{i,p-2}^{(n)}+q^{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\prod_{j;\;d(j,i)=1}\bigl(v_{i,p-1}^{(n)}\bigr)^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}}\end{split}

for some
{\mspace{1mu}\alpha},{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
, where

(8.32)

\displaystyle q^{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\left(v_{i,p+2}^{(n+1)}\cdot v_{i,p-2}^{(n)}\right)*\bigl(v_{i,p}^{(n)}\bigr)^{-1}\ \ \text{and}\ \ q^{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\left(\prod_{j;\;d(j,i)=1}\bigl(v_{i,p-1}^{(n)}\bigr)^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}}\right)*\bigl(v_{i,p}^{(n)}\bigr)^{-1}\text{ are bar-invariant.}

Here the dot product
\cdot
is given in (
4.8
).

The rest of this proof is devoted to show that the above quantum exchange relation coincides with the truncated image of the quantum folded
T
-system in Theorem
6.9
.
For this, it suffices to assume that
s=0
and hence
p\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\leqslant 0}
.
For each
(i,p)\in{}^{0}{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
, we set
k\mathbin{:=}\max(u\ |\ p+2u\leqslant 0)
.
By the induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle u_{i,p}^{(n+1)}*{{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k,p-2n})

\displaystyle=q^{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\left({{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k-1,p-2n})\cdot{{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k+1,p-2n-2})\right)+q^{\gamma}\hskip-8.61108pt\prod_{j;\;d(j,i)=1}\hskip-8.61108pt{{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}_{k,p-1-2n})^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}}

On the other hand, the corresponding truncated image of the quantum folded
T
-system in Theorem
6.9
is

(8.33)

\displaystyle{{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k,p-2n-2})*{{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(m^{(i)}_{k,p-2n})

\displaystyle=q^{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{\prime}}\left({{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k-1,p-2n})\cdot{{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k+1,p-2n-2})\right)

\displaystyle+q^{\gamma^{\prime}}\prod_{j;\;d(j,i)=1}{{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}_{k,p-1-2n})^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}},

where

\gamma^{\prime}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{b}}_{i,i}(2k-1)+\widetilde{\mathsf{b}}_{i,i}(2k+1)\right)\quad\text{ and }\quad{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{\prime}=\gamma^{\prime}+d_{i}.

By using the dominant monomials in (
8.33
) and bar-invariance in (
8.32
),

\displaystyle q^{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\bigl(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k-1,p-2n}\cdot\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k+1,p-2n-2}\bigr)*\bigl(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k,p-2n}\bigr)^{-1}\,\,\text{ and }\,\,q^{\gamma}\left(\prod_{j;\;d(j,i)=1}\bigl(\underline{m}^{(j)}_{k,p-1-2n}\bigr)^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}}\right)*\bigl(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k,p-2n}\bigr)^{-1}

are bar-invariant. Thus we have

\displaystyle{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\left(\sum_{b=0}^{k-2}\bigl(\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2(a-b)+1)-\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2(a-b)-1)\bigr)+\sum_{b=0}^{k}\bigl(\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2(a-b)+3)-\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2(a-b)+1)\bigr)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\left(\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2a+1)-\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2a-2k+3)+\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2a+3)-\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2a-2k+1)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2k+1)+\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2k-1)\right)+\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(1)={\mspace{1mu}\alpha}^{\prime}

and

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j;d(i,j)=1}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\left(\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\left(\sum_{b=0}^{k-1}\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2(a-b)+2)-\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2(a-b))\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j;d(i,j)=1}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\left(\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\left(\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2a+2)-\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2a-2k+2)\right)\right)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j;d(i,j)=1}-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2k)

\displaystyle\overset{\dagger}{=}\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2k+1)+\widetilde{\eta}_{i,i}(2k-1)\right)=\gamma^{\prime}.

Here
\overset{\dagger}{=}
holds by Lemma
2.3
.

Since
{{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(m^{(i)}_{k,p-2n})

is invertible in the skew-field of fractions
{{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{F}_{q}

of the quantum torus
{{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{X}_{q}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle v_{i,p}^{(n+1)}={{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108ptF_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k,p-2n-2}),

as desired. The second assertion follows from Proposition
5.25
.
∎

Let
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{T}_{q}
be the quantum torus associated with
{}^{s}L
generated by
v_{i,p}
for
(i,p)\in{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
.
Then,
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{T}_{q}
is isomorphic to
{}^{s}\mathcal{X}_{q}
.
Thus,
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{A}_{q}
can be understood as a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
-subalgebra in
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{T}_{q}
.

By following the argument in the proof of
[
5
, Lemma 6.4.1]
, we have the following lemma:

Lemma 8.6
.

The assignment

\displaystyle\Upomega:v_{i,p}\mapsto F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[p,s])

extends to a well-defined injective
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
-algebra homomorphism

\displaystyle\Upomega:{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{T}_{q}\mapsto\mathcal{X}_{q}.

Moreover, the restriction of
\Upomega
to the quantum cluster algebra
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{A}_{q}
has its image in the
quantum torus
\mathcal{X}_{q}
and the
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
-algebra homomorphisms
\Upomega
and
(\cdot)_{\leqslant s}
satisfy the following
commutative diagram:

where
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\Upomega
is the map induced from the assignment
v_{i,p}\to\underline{m}^{(i)}[p,s]
.

Let
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptR_{q}
be the image of the quantum cluster algebra
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{A}_{q}
under the map
\Upomega
:

R_{q,s}\mathbin{:=}\Upomega({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{A}_{q}).

Proposition 8.7
.

(cf.
[
30
, Theorem 5.1]
,
[
5
, Proposition 6.4.2]
)
We have

R_{q,s}=\mathfrak{K}_{q,s}(\mathfrak{g}).

Proof.

Let us recall
v_{i,p}\mathbin{:=}\underline{m^{(i)}[p,s]}
and
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{i}\in\{s-1,s\}
.
By Proposition
8.5
and Lemma
8.6
, we have

\displaystyle\Omega\left(v_{i,{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi_{i}}^{(n)}\right)=F_{q}\left(\mathsf{X}_{i,{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi_{i}-2n}\right)\quad\text{for $i\in{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}_{0}$ and $n\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}$}.

Since
\mathfrak{K}_{q,s}(\mathfrak{g})
is generated by
F_{q}\left(\mathsf{X}_{i,p}\right)
for all
(i,p)\in{}^{s}{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}
as a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
-algebra by Theorem
5.23
(see also (
5.21
) below), we have the following inclusion:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{q,s}(\mathfrak{g})\subset R_{q,s}.

Next, let us prove the reverse inclusion. As we see in Section
4.2
, there exist
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
-derivations
S_{i,q}:\mathcal{X}_{q}\to\mathcal{X}_{i,q}

such that

(8.38)

\begin{split}\bigcap_{i\in\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-2.77277pt\raisebox{0.54247pt}{{\scalebox{.38}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}\hskip 2.84526pt}}_{0}}{\rm Ker}(S_{i,q})=\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g}).\end{split}

Let us prove by induction that all cluster variables
Z
in
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{A}_{q}
satisfy
\Upomega(Z)\in\mathfrak{K}_{q,s}(\mathfrak{g})
.
Let
Z
be a quantum cluster variable in
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{A}_{q}
. If
Z
belongs to the initial cluster variables, it is done by definition of
\Upomega
.
Let us assume that
Z
does not belong to the initial cluster variables.
Then
Z
is obtained from a finite sequence of mutations. Then we have

\displaystyle ZZ_{1}=q^{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}M_{1}+q^{\mspace{1mu}\beta}M_{2},

where
Z_{1}
,

M_{1}
and
M_{2}
are quantum cluster monomials of
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathcal{A}_{q}
.
By the induction hypothesis,

(8.39)

\displaystyle\Upomega(Z_{1}),\,\,\,\Upomega(M_{1}),\,\,\,\Upomega(M_{2})\in\mathfrak{K}_{q,s}(\mathfrak{g}).

Note that
\Upomega(Z_{1})\neq 0
.
By Lemma
8.6
, we have

\displaystyle\Upomega(Z)*\Upomega(Z_{1})=q^{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}\Upomega(M_{1})+q^{\mspace{1mu}\beta}\Upomega(M_{2}).

Since
S_{i,q}
(
i\in{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}_{0}
) is a
\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}[q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}]
-linear derivation (Proposition
4.8
),

\displaystyle S_{i,q}(\Upomega(Z)*\Upomega(Z_{1}))

\displaystyle=\Upomega(Z){\bm{\cdot}}S_{i,q}(\Upomega(Z_{1}))+S_{i,q}(\Upomega(Z)){\bm{\cdot}}\Upomega(Z_{1})

\displaystyle=q^{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}S_{i,q}(\Upomega(M_{1}))+q^{\mspace{1mu}\beta}S_{i,q}(\Upomega(M_{2})).

By the induction hypothesis and (
8.38
), we have

\displaystyle S_{i,q}(\Upomega(Z_{1}))=S_{i,q}(\Upomega(M_{1}))=S_{i,q}(\Upomega(M_{2}))=0.

Then Lemma
4.7
tells us that

S_{i,q}(\Upomega(Z))=0
, that is,
\Upomega(Z)\in\mathfrak{K}_{i,q}(\mathfrak{g})
for all
i\in{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}_{0}
. Hence,

\Upomega(Z)\in\mathfrak{K}_{q,s}(\mathfrak{g})
due to (
8.38
) and (
8.39
), as we desired.
∎

Now, we present the main result in this section.

Theorem 8.8
.

For each heigh function
\xi
on
{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}
,

\mathfrak{K}_{q,\xi}(\mathfrak{g})
has a quantum cluster algebra structure
whose initial quantum seed is

(8.40)

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{\xi}=\bigl(\{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[p,\xi_{i}])\}_{(i,p)\in{{}^{\xi}}\hskip-0.60275pt\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-2.77277pt\raisebox{0.54247pt}{{\scalebox{.38}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}\hskip 2.84526pt}}_{0}},{{}^{\xi}}\hskip-0.86108ptL,{{}^{\xi}}\hskip-0.86108pt\widetilde{B}\bigr).

Proof.

Our assertion for
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi
already holds by Proposition
8.7
.
Let
j\in{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
be a source of
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi
. Then we have

(8.41)

\displaystyle{}_{{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi}^{\hskip 10.52737pt\textbf{{j}}}\upmu\left(\mathscr{S}_{{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi}\right)=\left(\{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[p-2\delta_{i,j},{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{i}-2\delta_{i,j}])\}_{(i,p)\in{}^{s}\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-2.77277pt\raisebox{0.54247pt}{{\scalebox{.38}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}\hskip 2.84526pt}}_{0}},{}^{s_{j}{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi}\hskip-0.86108ptL,{}^{s_{j}{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi}\hskip-0.86108pt\widetilde{B}\right)=\mathscr{S}_{s_{j}{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi},

by Proposition
8.2
and Proposition
8.5
.
Let
Q
(resp.
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108ptQ
) be the Dynkin quiver of
{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}
corresponding to
\xi
(resp.
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi
).
Since
any Dynkin quivers of
{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}
are connected by a finite sequence of reflections (up to constant on their height functions), so are
Q
and
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108ptQ
.
Then the quantum seed
\mathscr{S}_{{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi}
is mutation equivalent
to
\mathscr{S}_{\xi}
by (
8.41
) and
\mathsf{T}_{r}

(r\in 2\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu})
.
Hence, it follows from Lemma
7.5
and Proposition
8.7
that

\mathfrak{K}_{q,s}(\mathfrak{g})\simeq\mathscr{A}_{q^{1/2}}(\mathscr{S}_{{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.60275pt\xi})\simeq\mathscr{A}_{q^{1/2}}(\mathscr{S}_{\xi})\simeq\mathfrak{K}_{q,\xi}(\mathfrak{g})
, so
\mathfrak{K}_{q,\xi}(\mathfrak{g})
has a quantum cluster algebra structure.
∎

As an application of Theorem
8.8
, we obtain
q
-commutativities of
F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k,r})
and
F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}_{l,t})
satisfying certain conditions as follows.

Theorem 8.9
.

For a pair
(m^{(i)}_{k,r},\,m^{(j)}_{l,t})
,

\bigl(F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k,r}),F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}_{l,t})\bigr)
is a
q
-commuting pair if

(1)

r-d(i,j)\leqslant t\leqslant t+2(l-1)\leqslant r+2(k-1)+d(i,j)
or

(2)

t-d(i,j)\leqslant r\leqslant r+2(k-1)\leqslant t+2(l-1)+d(i,j)
.

In particular,
F_{q}(m^{(i)}_{k,r})
q-commutes with
F_{q}(X_{j,p})
if

r-d(i,j)\leqslant p\leqslant r+2(k-1)+d(i,j).

Proof.

Under the conditions
1
and
2
, there exists a height function
\xi
on
{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}
such that
\xi_{i}=r+2(k-1)
and
\xi_{j}=t+2(l-1)
.
Then we have

\displaystyle F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k,r})=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[\xi_{i}-2(k-1),\xi_{i}])\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}_{l,t})=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[\xi_{j}-2(l-1),\xi_{j}])

which can be viewed as initial quantum cluster variables in
\mathscr{S}_{\xi}
.
Thus our assertion follows from Theorem
8.8
.
∎

The conjecture below is proved in
[
61
]
when
\mathfrak{g}
is of finite
AD
-type.

Conjecture 4
.

For a pair
(m^{(i)}_{k,r},\,m^{(j)}_{l,t})
,

F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}_{k,r})
and
F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}_{l,t})

q
-commute unless there exist
1\leqslant u\leqslant\mathsf{h}
and
0\leqslant s\leqslant\min(k,l)-1
satisfying

(8.42)

|k+r-l-t|=u+|k-l|+2s\quad\text{ and }\quad\widetilde{\mathsf{b}}_{i,j}(u-1)\neq 0.

9.
Extension to
\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})

In this section,
we will extend Theorem
8.8
to
\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
, that is, the quantum virtual Grothendieck ring
\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
has also a quantum cluster algebra structure (of skew-symmetrizable type) isomorphic to its subalgebra
\mathfrak{K}_{q,\xi}(\mathfrak{g})
for each height function
\xi
on
{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}
.

9.1.
Sink-source quiver

For an integer
s\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
, recall the height function
{}^{(s)}\xi
on
{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}
.
Now let us consider a new valued quiver
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}
whose set of vertices is
{}^{s}{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
and the exchange matrix
{}^{s}\mathfrak{B}
is given as follows:

(9.1)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{(i,p),(j,t)}=\begin{cases}-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}&\text{ if either (a) $t-p=1$, $i\neq j$ and $p\equiv_{4}\xi_{i}=s-1$},\\
&\text{ $\hskip 21.09712pt$ or (b) $p-t=1$, $i\neq j$ and $p\not\equiv_{4}\xi_{i}=s$,}\\
\mathsf{c}_{i,j}&\text{ if either (a${}^{\prime}$) $p-t=1$, $i\neq j$ and $p\equiv_{4}\xi_{i}=s$},\\
&\text{ $\hskip 21.09712pt$ or (b${}^{\prime}$) $t-p=1$, $i\neq j$ and $p\not\equiv_{4}\xi_{i}=s-1$,}\\
1&\text{ if either (A) $|p-t|=2$, $i=j$ and $p\equiv_{4}\xi_{i}=s$, }\\
&\text{ $\hskip 21.09712pt$ or (B) $|p-t|=2$, $i=j$ and $p\not\equiv_{4}\xi_{i}=s-1$,}\\
-1&\text{ if either (A${}^{\prime}$) $|p-t|=2$, $i=j$ and $p\not\equiv_{4}\xi_{i}=s$, }\\
&\text{ $\hskip 21.09712pt$ or (B${}^{\prime}$) $|p-t|=2$, $i=j$ and $p\equiv_{4}\xi_{i}=s-1$,}\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Note that
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{B}
satisfies (
2.12
) with the sequence

S=(s_{i,p}\;|\;s_{i,p}=d_{i})
and without frozen vertices.

.

for non-simply-laced types:

and
\mathfrak{g}

Remark 9.2
.

Note that every vertex
(i,p)
in
{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}_{0}
is either

(1)

vertically sink and horizontally source, or

(2)

vertically source and horizontally sink.

More precisely, when

(1)

\xi_{i}=s
and
p\equiv_{4}s
, or
\xi_{i}=(s-1)
and
p\not\equiv_{4}s-1
,
(i,p)
satisfies (i),

(2)

\xi_{i}=s
and
p\not\equiv_{4}s
, or
\xi_{i}=(s-1)
and
p\equiv_{4}s-1
,
(i,p)
satisfies (ii).

For each
(i,p)\in{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}_{0}={}^{s}{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
, we assign

{}^{s}\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}\in\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
at
(i,p)
, which is defined by

(9.2)

{}^{s}\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}\mathbin{:=}F_{q}\left(\underline{m}^{(i)}[{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{i,p},{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{i,p}+2\;{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptl_{i,p}]\right),

where

(9.3)

{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptl_{i,p}\mathbin{:=}\left({}^{(s)}\xi_{i}-p\right)/2\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}\quad\text{ and }\quad{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{i,p}\mathbin{:=}{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{i}-2\times\left\lfloor\frac{{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptl_{i,p}+\delta\bigl({}^{(s)}\xi_{i}=s\bigr)}{2}\right\rfloor.

.

, we have

\textstyle{F_{q}(m^{(2)}[-1,-1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{3\urcorner}

Let us define a matrix
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\Uplambda=({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\Uplambda_{(i,p),(j,t)})_{(i,p),(j,t)\in{}^{s}\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-2.77277pt\raisebox{0.54247pt}{{\scalebox{.38}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}\hskip 2.84526pt}}_{0}}
such that

{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\Uplambda_{(i,p),(j,t)}=\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(m^{(i)}[{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{i,p},{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{i,p}+2\;{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptl_{i,p}],m^{(j)}[{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{j,t},{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{j,s}+2\;{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptl_{j,t}]).

Theorem 9.4
.

The pair
({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\Uplambda,{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{B})
is compatible with
{\rm diag}(2d_{i,p}\mathbin{:=}2d_{i}\,\mid\,(i,p)\in{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.87111pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.67998pt\raisebox{6.63951pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.87111pt}}}_{0})
.

Proof.

Let
(i,p),(j,t)\in{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}_{0}
.
In this proof, we only consider the case of
\xi_{j}=s
and
t\equiv_{4}\xi_{j}
, since the other cases are similar.
Set
a_{1}={{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{i,p}
,
a_{2}=a_{1}+2{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptl_{i,p}
,
b_{1}={{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{j,t}
and
b_{2}=b_{1}+2{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptl_{l,t}
.
By (
9.1
), we have

\begin{split}-({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\Uplambda{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{B})_{(i,p),(j,t)}&=\delta(t\neq\xi_{j})\;{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\Uplambda_{(i,p),(j,t+2)}+{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\Uplambda_{(i,p),(j,t-2)}+\sum_{k;\;d(j,k)=1}\mathsf{c}_{k,j}{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\Uplambda_{(i,p),(k,t-1)}\\
&=\delta(t\neq\xi_{j}){}^{s}\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(m^{(i)}[a_{1},a_{2}],m^{(j)}[b_{1},b_{2}-2])+\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(m^{(i)}[a_{1},a_{2}],m^{(j)}[b_{1}-2,b_{2}])\\
&\hskip 10.76385pt+\sum_{k;\;d(j,k)=1}\mathsf{c}_{k,j}\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(m^{(i)}[a_{1},a_{2}],m^{(k)}[b_{1}-1,b_{2}-1])\\
&\overset{*}{=}\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(m^{(i)}[a_{1},a_{2}],\,B_{j,b_{1}-1}B_{j,b_{1}+3}\cdots B_{j,b_{2}-1})\end{split}

where
\overset{*}{=}
holds by (
3.6
) and (
4.4
).
Then
it follows from (
4.9
) in Proposition
4.6
that

\displaystyle-({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\Uplambda{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{B})_{(i,p),(j,t)}

\displaystyle=\mspace{1mu}\underline{\mathcal{N}}(m^{(i)}[a_{1},a_{2}],B_{j,b_{1}-1}B_{j,b_{1}+3}\cdots B_{j,b_{2}-1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=0}^{\frac{a_{2}-a_{1}}{2}}\sum_{y=0}^{\frac{b_{2}-b_{1}}{2}}\delta_{i,j}(-\delta(a_{1}+2x-b_{1}-2y=-2)+\delta(a_{1}+2x-b_{1}-2y=0))2d_{i}

\displaystyle=\delta_{i,j}\sum_{x=0}^{\frac{a_{2}-a_{1}}{2}}(-\delta(a_{1}+2x-b_{1}=-2)+\delta(a_{1}+2x-b_{2}=0))2d_{i}.

If
i=j
, we have the following:

(1)

[a_{1},a_{2}]
and
[b_{1},b_{2}]
are inclusive, that is, either

[a_{1},a_{2}]\subset[b_{1},b_{2}]\text{ or }[b_{1},b_{2}]\subset[a_{1},a_{2}]
;

(2)

if
a_{k}=b_{k}
, then
b_{l}-a_{l}=2
or
0
for
\{k,l\}=\{1,2\}
.

Thus we can conclude that

\displaystyle-({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\Uplambda{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{B})_{(i,p),(j,t)}=\delta((i,p)=(j,t))2d_{i},

as we desired.
∎

Lemma 9.5
.

The set
\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}\}_{\in{}^{s}\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-2.77277pt\raisebox{0.54247pt}{{\scalebox{.38}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}\hskip 2.84526pt}}_{0}}
forms a
q
-commuting family in
\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
.

Proof.

From Theorem
8.9
, our assertion easily follows.
∎

Theorem 9.6
.

The family of quantum seeds

(9.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{s}=(\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}\}_{(i,p)\in{}^{s}\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-2.77277pt\raisebox{0.54247pt}{{\scalebox{.38}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}\hskip 2.84526pt}}_{0}},{}^{s}\Uplambda,{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{B})\quad\text{for $s\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}$,}

gives a
quantum cluster algebra structure on
\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
.

The rest of this paper will be devoted to proving Theorem
9.6
.
Let
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathscr{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
be the quantum cluster algebra generated by the quantum seed
\mathfrak{S}_{s}
.
To prove Theorem
9.6
, we need to show that

(9.5)

{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathscr{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})=\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g}).

Then the proof of (
9.5
) is separated into two steps as follows:

Step 1
.
For the inclusion
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathscr{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})\subset\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
, we will prove the following proposition in Section
9.2
.

Proposition 9.7
.

For any finite sequence
\mu
of mutations, a cluster variable in
\mu\bigl(\mathfrak{S}_{s}\bigr)
is contained in
\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
.

The key observation for proving Proposition
9.7
is that the mutated variables from
\mathfrak{S}_{s}
are understood as the ones from
\mathscr{S}_{s^{\prime}}
for some
s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
, which implies
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathscr{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})\subset\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
.

Step 2
. The opposite inclusion will be proved as the following proposition is shown in Section
9.3
.

Proposition 9.8
.

For
(i,p)\in{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
, there exists a finite sequence
\mu
of mutations such that

\mu\bigl(\mathfrak{S}_{s}\bigr)

contains
F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{i,p})
as its cluster variable.

Since
\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
is generated by
F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{i,p})
for
(i,p)\in{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
by Theorem
5.21
(see also (
5.21
) below),
the opposite inclusion for proving (
9.5
) follows from Proposition
9.8
.

9.2.
Proof of Theorem
9.6
: Step 1

For
k\leqslant s
, we set

\displaystyle\bm{\langle}{k}\bm{\rangle}\mathbin{:=}\{(i,k)\in{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}\}\quad\text{ and }\quad\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}\mathbin{:=}\{(i,p)\in{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}\ |\ k\leqslant p\leqslant s\}.

We understand
\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}=\emptyset
for
k>s
.

Lemma 9.9
.

For the valued quiver
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mu_{(i_{1},s)}\circ\mu_{(i_{2},s)}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{(i_{r},s)}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})\simeq\mu_{(j_{1},s)}\circ\mu_{(j_{2},s)}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{(j_{r},s)}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}),

where

\{(i_{t},s)\}_{1\leqslant t\leqslant r}=\{(j_{t},s)\}_{1\leqslant t\leqslant r}=\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle}
.
Thus,
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle}}
is well-defined on
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}
, that is,
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})
is uniquely determined.

Proof.

Note that (a) each
(i_{k},s)\in\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle}
is vertically sink and horizontally source, (b) all the length 2 paths passing through
(i_{k},s)

start from
(i^{\prime},s-1)
and end at
(i_{k},s-2)
where
d(i^{\prime},i_{k})=1
, and
(c) there is no arrow between
(i_{k},s)
and
(i_{k^{\prime}},s)
for
i_{k}\neq i_{k^{\prime}}
.

\displaystyle{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}=\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{35.00005pt}{ Thus the mutation
\mu_{(i_{k},s)}
of
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}
at
(i_{k},s)
does not affect the local circumstance of
(i_{k^{\prime}},s)
and the arrows between
(i_{k},s-2)
and
(i^{\prime},s-1)
for
d(i_{k},i^{\prime})=1
are canceled out by the mutation
\mu_{(i_{k},s)}
.

Hence the assertions follow.
∎

Lemma 9.10
.

For the valued quiver
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}
and
k\leqslant s
, the valued quiver

(9.7)

\displaystyle\mu_{\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})\mathbin{:=}\mu_{\bm{\langle}{k}\bm{\rangle}}\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{k+1}\bm{\rangle}}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})\text{ is uniquely determined}.

Thus
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{k}\bm{\rangle}}
is well-defined on
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{k+1,s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})
and hence
\mu_{\langle k,s\rangle}
is well-defined on
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}
.

Proof.

The assertion for
k=s
holds by the previous lemma. As we can observe in (
9.6
), (a) each
(i^{\prime},s-1)\in\bm{\langle}{s-1}\bm{\rangle}
is vertically sink and horizontally source,
(b) all the length 2 paths passing through
(i^{\prime},s-1)

start from
(i,s)
and end at
(i^{\prime},s-3)
where
d(i^{\prime},i)=1
and
(c) there is no path between
(i^{\prime},s-1)
and
(i^{\prime\prime},s-1)
. Thus
\mu_{(i^{\prime},s-1)}\circ\mu_{(i^{\prime\prime},s-1)}=\mu_{(i^{\prime\prime},s-1)}\circ\mu_{(i^{\prime},s-1)}
on
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})
. Thus the assertion holds for
k=s-1
, and

\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-1}\bm{\rangle}}
yields arrows from
(i,s)
to
(i^{\prime},s-3)
, and hence
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-1,s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})
can be depicted as follows:

\textstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{(i_{k},s-4)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i_{k},i^{\prime}},\mathsf{c}_{i^{\prime},i_{k}}\lrcorner}
\textstyle{(i_{k},s-2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(i_{k},s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i_{k},i^{\prime}},\mathsf{c}_{i^{\prime},i_{k}}\lrcorner}
\textstyle{(i^{\prime},s-5)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i^{\prime},i_{k^{\prime}}},\mathsf{c}_{i_{k^{\prime}},i^{\prime}}\lrcorner}

\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i^{\prime},i_{k^{\prime}}},\mathsf{c}_{i_{k^{\prime}},i^{\prime}}\lrcorner}

\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i_{k^{\prime}},i^{\prime}},\mathsf{c}_{i^{\prime},i_{k^{\prime}}}\lrcorner}

By the same reasons for

and
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-1}\bm{\rangle}}
, the sequence of mutations
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2}\bm{\rangle}}
is well-defined. Furthermore, by the mutation rules, the arrows between

for

are canceled out by the mutation
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-1}\bm{\rangle}}
. Thus
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2,s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})
can be depicted as follows:

(1)

the full-subquiver of
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2,s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})
obtained by excluding vertices in
\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle}
is isomorphic to the valued quiver
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{\triangle}})
in (
9.6
),

(2)

the full-subquiver of
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-3,s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})
obtained by excluding vertices in
\bm{\langle}{s-1,s}\bm{\rangle}
is isomorphic to the valued quiver
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-1,s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{\triangle}})
in (
9.2
).

Thus the induction works.
∎

For notational simplicity, let us keep the following notations:

•

\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle})\mathbin{:=}\mu_{\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})
(in (
9.7
)),
\Upsilon_{s}\mathbin{:=}{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}
,
\Theta_{s}\mathbin{:=}{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}
,
\Omega_{s}\mathbin{:=}{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\overset{\to}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}
,

•

for a valued quiver
\Upgamma
, a quiver
{}^{X}\Upgamma
denotes the full-subquiver of
\Upgamma
whose vertices are in
X\subseteq\Upgamma_{0}
,

where
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\overset{\to}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}
is the quiver obtained from
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}
by reversing the orientation of arrows in
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}
.
By Remark
9.11

2
, we have

(9.32)

\displaystyle{}^{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,k-3}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle})\simeq{}^{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,k-3}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s},

for any
k\leqslant s
.
The lemma below concerns
{}^{\bm{\langle}{k-3,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle})
.

Lemma 9.13
.

For
r\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}
, as a finite quiver,

(1)

{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+1,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-2r+1,s}\bm{\rangle})\simeq{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+1,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}
.

(2)

{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+1,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle})\simeq{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+1,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}
and

{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r-3,s-2r+2}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle})\simeq\begin{cases}{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-3,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Omega_{s}&\text{ if }r=0,\\
{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-5,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Theta_{s}&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

1

Recall
\bm{\langle}{s-2r+1,s}\bm{\rangle}=\emptyset
if
r=0
, so this case trivially holds.
The cases of
r=1
and
r=2
are already verified in (
9.2
) and (
9.2
), respectively.
One observes that in the general case (i.e.
r\geqslant 3
), the mutation patterns in the intermediate steps are identical with (
9.6
) and (
9.2
) up to the shift of the second parameters.
This completes the proof of
1
.

2

Let us consider the cases of
0\leqslant r\leqslant 2
precisely as follows:

Case 1
.
r=0
.
By (
9.6
),
{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-3,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle})
and
{}^{\bm{\langle}{s+1,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle})
are

•

{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+1,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle})\simeq{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+1,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}

For
r\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 1}
, we define

\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle}\hskip-1.80835pt\bm{\rangle}}\mathbin{:=}\begin{cases}\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle}}\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-4,s}\bm{\rangle}}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+4,s}\bm{\rangle}}\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle}}&\text{ if }r\equiv_{2}0,\\
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2,s}\bm{\rangle}}\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-6,s}\bm{\rangle}}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+4,s}\bm{\rangle}}\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle}}&\text{ if }r\equiv_{2}1.\end{cases}

By Lemma
9.13

2
,

{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+1,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle})\simeq{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+1,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}
.
By Lemma
9.10
and Remark
9.11

2
,

\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+4,s}\bm{\rangle}}
is well-defined on
{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+1,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle})
.
Thus it makes sense to define

\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle}\hskip-2.58334pt\bm{\rangle})\mathbin{:=}\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle}\hskip-1.80835pt\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}).

Then we have a generalization of Lemma
9.13
.

Proposition 9.14
.

For
r\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}
,
we have

{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r-3,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle}\hskip-2.58334pt\bm{\rangle})\simeq\begin{cases}{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r-3,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Omega_{s}&\text{ if }r\equiv_{2}0,\\
{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r-3,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Theta_{s}&\text{ if }r\equiv_{2}1.\end{cases}

Proof.

We first consider the case
0\leqslant r\leqslant 2
, and then the general case
r\geqslant 3
.

Case 1
.
0\leqslant r\leqslant 2
.
The assertion for
r=0
and
r=1
is shown by (
9.6
) and (
9.2
), respectively.
Let us consider the case
r=2
.
By (
9.12
),
we may consider

{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-7,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-4,s}\bm{\rangle})

by separating it into two parts
{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-7,s-2}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-4,s}\bm{\rangle})
and
{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-4,s}\bm{\rangle})
,
where each one is shown in (
9.2
).
Then
{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-7,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-4,s}\bm{\rangle}\hskip-2.58334pt\bm{\rangle})
is understood as a concatenation of
{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-7,s-2}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-4,s}\bm{\rangle})

and
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle}}\bigl({}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-4,s}\bm{\rangle})\bigr)
due to Remark
9.11

2
, where the common vertices are overlapped.
Since
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s}\bm{\rangle}}\bigl({}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{s-4,s}\bm{\rangle})\bigr)
is isomorphic to the valued quiver in (
9.2
), the assertion for
r=2
is proved.

Case 2
.
r\geqslant 3
.
The proof idea in this case is identical with
Case 1
, that is,
by using the same argument as in
Case 1
, we observe that the finite valued quiver

\Upgamma_{1}\mathbin{:=}{}^{\bm{\langle}{s-2r-3,s-2r+6}\bm{\rangle}}\left(\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2r+4,s}\bm{\rangle}}\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{s-2r,s}\bm{\rangle}}({{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}})\right)

is a concatenation of

\textstyle{(i_{k},s-2r-2)}
\textstyle{(i_{k},s-2r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i^{\prime},i_{k^{\prime}}},\mathsf{c}_{i_{k^{\prime}},i^{\prime}}\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i^{\prime},i_{k}},\mathsf{c}_{i_{k},i^{\prime}}\lrcorner}
\textstyle{(i^{\prime},s-2r-1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i_{k^{\prime}},i^{\prime}},\mathsf{c}_{i^{\prime},i_{k^{\prime}}}\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i_{k},i^{\prime}},\mathsf{c}_{i^{\prime},i_{k}}\lrcorner}
\textstyle{(i^{\prime},s-2r+1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i^{\prime},i_{k^{\prime}}},\mathsf{c}_{i_{k^{\prime}},i^{\prime}}\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i^{\prime},i_{k}},\mathsf{c}_{i_{k},i^{\prime}}\lrcorner}
\textstyle{(i_{k^{\prime}},s-2r-2)}
\textstyle{(i_{k^{\prime}},s-2r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(i_{k^{\prime}},s-2r+2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

and

and

does not contribute to
\Upgamma_{1}
, we complete the proof
by applying the same argument to

as in
Case 1
.
∎

Let us write
\mu
in Proposition
9.7
as

(9.51)

\displaystyle\mu=\mu_{(i_{l},p_{l})}\circ\mu_{(i_{l-1},p_{l-1})}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{(i_{1},p_{1})}.

Take
t\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
such that
t\ll\min(p_{k}\ |\ 1\leqslant k\leqslant l)
and
s-t\equiv_{4}2
.
By our choice of
t
, it follows from Proposition
9.14
that

\displaystyle{}^{\bm{\langle}{t-3,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Upsilon_{s}(\bm{\langle}{t,s}\bm{\rangle}\hskip-2.58334pt\bm{\rangle})\simeq{}^{\bm{\langle}{t-3,s}\bm{\rangle}}\Theta_{s}\quad\text{ as a valued quiver,}

where

(9.52)

\displaystyle s-t=4u+2\qquad\text{ for some $u\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}$.}

Recall the quantum seeds

(9.53)

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{s}=\bigl(\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{v}_{i,p}\mathbin{:=}F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[p,{}^{(s)}\xi_{i}])\}_{(i,p)\in{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.60275pt\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-2.77277pt\raisebox{0.54247pt}{{\scalebox{.38}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}\hskip 2.84526pt}}_{0}},{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptL,{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\widetilde{B}\bigr)\text{ associated to ${}^{(s)}\xi$ in~\eqref{eq: scr seed S}},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{s}=\left(\left\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}=F_{q}\bigl(\underline{m}^{(i)}[{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{i,p},{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{i,p}+2\;{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108ptl_{i,p}]\bigr)\right\}_{(i,p)\in{}^{s}\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-2.77277pt\raisebox{0.54247pt}{{\scalebox{.38}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}\hskip 2.84526pt}}_{0}},{}^{s}\Uplambda,{}^{s}\mathfrak{B}\right)\text{ in ~\eqref{eq: qseed frak}.}

Proposition 9.15
.

Every mutation
\mu_{(i,p)}
in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{t,s}\bm{\rangle}\hskip-1.80835pt\bm{\rangle}}
on the cluster
\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{v}_{i,p}\}
corresponds to the quantum folded T-system in Theorem
6.9
. Furthermore, each mutation
\mu_{(i,p)}
of the quantum cluster variable sitting at
(i,p)
corresponds to
\mathsf{T}_{-2}
.

Proof.

First, let us consider a mutation
\mu_{(i,p)}
in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{t,s}\bm{\rangle}}
.
When
(i,p)=(i,s)
(i.e. one of the vertices located in the right-most of
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
), the local circumstance of
(i,s)
described in Remark
9.11

1
tells us that the quantum exchange relation is given by

\displaystyle\mu_{(i,s)}\big(F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{i,s})\big)*F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{i,s})

\displaystyle=q^{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}(i,1)}F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[s-2,s])+q^{\gamma(i,1)}\prod_{j;\;d_{i,j}=1}F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{j,s-1})^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}},

where
q^{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}(i,1)}
and
q^{\gamma(i,1)}
are determined to be bar-invariant as in the sense of (
8.31
).
Consequently, it corresponds to the quantum folded T-system in Theorem
6.9
and hence
\mu_{(i,s)}(F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{i,s}))=F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{i,s-2})
as we desired.
Note that another mutation at
(i^{\prime},s)
does not affect the mutation at
(i,s)
as shown in Lemma
9.9
.

Second, let us consider a mutation at
(j,s-1)
, which appears later than any
(i,s)
in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{t,s}\bm{\rangle}}
.
Let us keep in mind that the cluster variable located at
(i^{\prime},s)
is already mutated by former mutations, which is
F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{i^{\prime},s-2})
.
Then the quantum exchange relation is given as follows (recall Remark
9.11

1
):

\displaystyle\mu_{(j,s-1)}\big(F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{j,s-1})\big)*F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{j,s-1})

\displaystyle=q^{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}(j,1)}F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}[s-3,s-1])+q^{\gamma(j,1)}\prod_{i;\;d_{j,i}=1}F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{i,s-2})^{-\mathsf{c}_{i,j}},

which coincides with the quantum folded T-system in Theorem
6.9
.
Hence
\mu_{(j,s-1)}(F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{j,s-1}))=F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{j,s-3})
, as we desired.

Finally, by using this argument and the local circumstance of
(k,p)
in the order for applying
\mu_{(k,p)}
, described in Remark
9.11

1
, one can conclude that each mutation
\mu_{(i,p)}
in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{t,s}\bm{\rangle}}
corresponds to shifting the second parameters of cluster variables by
-2
.
The assertion for mutations in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{t+4r,s}\bm{\rangle}}

(r\geqslant 1)
follows from Lemma
9.13

2
, Remark
9.11

2
and the argument for mutations in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{t,s}\bm{\rangle}}
.
∎

Recall
u\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\geqslant 0}
in (
9.52
) depending on
\bm{\langle}{t,s}\bm{\rangle}
. For
(j,a)\in{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
with
t\leqslant a\leqslant s
, we remark that

(A)

there exists
0\leqslant e\leqslant u

such that
s-4e-2\leqslant a<\min(s+1,s-4e+2)
, equivalently

a\in\{s-4e-2,s-4e-1,s-4e,s-4e+1\},

(B)

{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{j}=s
if
a=s-4e-2
or
s-4e
, and
{{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{j}=s-1
, otherwise,

(C)

since
\mu_{(j,a)}
appears
(u+1-e)
-times in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{t,s}\bm{\rangle}\hskip-1.80835pt\bm{\rangle}}
and
{}^{s}\mathfrak{v}_{j,a}=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}[a,s-\delta({{}^{(s)}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{j}\neq s)])
, it follows from Proposition
9.15
that

\bigl(\mu_{\bm{\langle}{t,s}\bm{\rangle}\hskip-1.80835pt\bm{\rangle}}(\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{v}_{k,p}\})\bigr)_{(j,a)}=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}[a^{\prime}+2e,s^{\prime}+2e-\delta(a\not\equiv_{2}s)]).

Proposition 9.16
.

For
(j,a)\in{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
with
t\leqslant a\leqslant s
,

\bigl(\mu_{\bm{\langle}{t,s}\bm{\rangle}\hskip-1.80835pt\bm{\rangle}}(\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{v}_{k,p}\})\bigr)_{(j,a)}=F_{q}\bigl(\underline{m}^{(j)}[{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{j,a^{\prime}},{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{j,a^{\prime}}+2{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108ptl_{j,a^{\prime}}]),

where
s^{\prime}=s-2(u+1)
and
a^{\prime}=a-2(u+1)
for
u\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
in
(C)
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\bigl({{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{j,a^{\prime}},{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108ptl_{j,a^{\prime}}\bigr)=\begin{cases}\bigl(s^{\prime}-2(e+1),2e+1\bigr)&\text{ if }s-a=4e+2,\\
\bigl(s^{\prime}-2e,2e\bigr)&\text{ if }s-a=4e,\\
\bigl(s^{\prime}-2e,2e\bigr)&\text{ if }(s-1)-a=4e,\\
\bigl(s^{\prime}-2(e-1),2e-1\bigr)&\text{ if }(s-1)-a=4e-2,\end{cases}

where the integers on the left-hand side are defined in (
9.3
).
Then one can easily check that

\displaystyle{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{j,a^{\prime}}=a^{\prime}+2e\quad\text{ and }\quad{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108pto_{j,a^{\prime}}+2\;{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108ptl_{j,a^{\prime}}=s^{\prime}+2e-\delta(a\not\equiv_{2}s),

which implies our assertion.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Proposition
9.7
.

Proof of Proposition
9.7
.

Write
\mu
in Proposition
9.7
as in (
9.51
).
Let us set

\displaystyle Z\mathbin{:=}(\mu(\{{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{u}_{k,p}\}))_{(i_{l},p_{l})}.

By Proposition
9.14
and Proposition
9.16
,
we have

\displaystyle(\{{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{v}_{k^{\prime},p^{\prime}}\})_{\bm{\langle}{t^{\prime},s^{\prime}}\bm{\rangle}}=(\{{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{u}_{k,p}\})_{\bm{\langle}{t,s}\bm{\rangle}},

that is,

Z
can be understood as a mutated variable from
\{{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{v}_{k^{\prime},p^{\prime}}\}
as follows:

\displaystyle Z=\bigl(\mu\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{t^{\prime},s^{\prime}}\bm{\rangle}\hskip-1.80835pt\bm{\rangle}}(\{{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{v}_{k^{\prime},p^{\prime}}\})\bigr)_{(i_{l},p_{l}^{\prime})},

Here
t^{\prime}=t+2(u+1)
,
s^{\prime}=s+2(u+1)
and
p_{l}^{\prime}=p_{l}+2(u+1)
.
Since

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{s^{\prime}}=\bigl(\{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[p,{{}^{(s^{\prime})}}\hskip-0.86108pt\xi_{i}])\}_{(i,p)\in{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.60275pt\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-2.77277pt\raisebox{0.54247pt}{{\scalebox{.38}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}\hskip 2.84526pt}}_{0}},{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108ptL,{{}^{s^{\prime}}}\hskip-0.86108pt\widetilde{B}\bigr)

is an initial quantum seed of the quantum cluster algebra
\mathfrak{K}_{q,s^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{g})\subset\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
,

Z
is contained in
\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
, which completes the proof.
∎

9.3.
Proof of Theorem
9.6
: Step 2

For
k\leqslant s
, we set

\displaystyle\bm{\langle}{k}\bm{\rangle}^{-}

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}\{(i,k)\in{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}_{0}\ |\ \text{ $(i,k)$ is vertically sink and horizontally source in ${}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}_{0}$ }\},

\displaystyle\bm{\langle}{k}\bm{\rangle}^{+}

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}\{(i,k)\in{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}_{0}\ |\ \text{ $(i,k)$ is vertically source and horizontally sink in ${}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}_{0}$ }\}.

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\leqslant s}\sqcup\{-\infty\}
,

\displaystyle\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{-}

\displaystyle\mathbin{:=}\bigsqcup_{k\leqslant t\leqslant s}\bm{\langle}{t}\bm{\rangle}^{-}\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}\mathbin{:=}\bigsqcup_{k\leqslant t\leqslant s}\bm{\langle}{t}\bm{\rangle}^{+}.

If
k>s
, then we understand those sets as empty set. Note that there is no arrows between vertices in
\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\leqslant s}\sqcup\{-\infty\}
.

Lemma 9.17
.

For
\{(i_{t},p_{t})\}_{1\leqslant t\leqslant r}=\{(j_{t},q_{t})\}_{1\leqslant t\leqslant r}=\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}
, as a valued quiver,

\displaystyle\mu_{(i_{1},p_{1})}\circ\mu_{(i_{2},p_{2})}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{(i_{r},p_{r})}({}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.87111pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.67998pt\raisebox{6.63951pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.87111pt}}})\simeq\mu_{(j_{1},q_{1})}\circ\mu_{(j_{2},q_{2})}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{(j_{r},q_{r})}({}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.87111pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.67998pt\raisebox{6.63951pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.87111pt}}}),

that is,
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}}({}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.87111pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.67998pt\raisebox{6.63951pt}{\rule{6.13336pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.87111pt}}})
is uniquely determined.

We remark that an analog of Lemma
9.17
by replacing
{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}
with
{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\to}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}
also holds.

Proof.

In this proof, we only consider the case of
\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}
since the proof of
\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{-}
is similar.
Let
(i,p),(j,s)\in\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}
such that
(i,p)\neq(j,s)
.
The neighborhood of
(i,p)
on the valued quiver
{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}
is depicted as follows:

\displaystyle{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern Here one can observe that

•

\mu_{(i,p)}({}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}})
has arrows between
(i,p\pm 2)
and
(j,p+1)
for
d(i,j)=1
, where
(i,p\pm 2),(j,p+1)\in\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{-}
,

•

the arrows adjacent to
(j,p-1)
and
(j,p+3)
are not changed by
\mu_{(i,p)}
.

Hence, for
(x,y)\in\{(j,p-1),(j,p+3)\ |\ d(i,j)=1\}
, the mutation
\mu_{(x,y)}\large(\mu_{(i,p)}({}^{s}\hskip-2.06664pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.25835pt]{0.4pt}{9.04745pt}}\hss}\kern 0.46991pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.22995pt\raisebox{7.63179pt}{\rule{7.04999pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{7.04999pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.46991pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.25835pt]{0.4pt}{9.04745pt}}})\large)

yields arrows between
(x,y\pm 2)
and
(k,y+1)
for
d(x,k)=1
, one of which disappears due to an arrow from
\mu_{(i,p)}({}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}})
.
For instance,

\displaystyle{\scriptsize\mu_{(j^{\prime},p-1)}(\mu_{(i,p)}({}^{s}\hskip-1.20552pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.1507pt]{0.4pt}{6.13866pt}}\hss}\kern 0.32771pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.87001pt\raisebox{5.17813pt}{\rule{4.78339pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{4.91673pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.32771pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.1507pt]{0.4pt}{6.13866pt}}}))}

0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(j^{\prime},p+3)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 451.55058pt\raise-46.14775pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.01945pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{j^{\prime},i},\mathsf{c}_{i,j^{\prime}}\lrcorner}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 455.56718pt\raise-38.76219pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 555.31952pt\raise-61.5333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 531.31953pt\raise-61.5333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}

Here the arrow from
(i,p-2)
to
(j^{\prime},p+1)
on
\mu_{(i,p)}({}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}})
disappeared by the new arrow from
(j^{\prime},p+1)
to
(i,p-2)
generated when we apply the mutation
\mu_{(j^{\prime},p-1)}
to
(\mu_{(i,p)}({}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}))
. In fact, one may observe that

\displaystyle\mu_{(i,p)}\circ\mu_{(j^{\prime},p-1)}=\mu_{(j^{\prime},p-1)}\circ\mu_{(i,p)}\,\,\text{ on }\,\,{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}},

and the arrows among
(i,p)
,
(i,p-2)
,
(j^{\prime},p+1)
and
(j^{\prime},p-1)
in
\mu_{(j^{\prime},p-1)}\circ\mu_{(i,p)}({}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}})
are reversed.
Furthermore, one may generalize the above as follows:

\displaystyle\mu_{(i,p)}\circ\mu_{(j,s)}=\mu_{(j,s)}\circ\mu_{(i,p)}\,\,\text{ on }\,\,\mu_{(i_{k},p_{k})}\circ\cdots\circ\mu_{(i_{r},p_{r})}({}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}})

for
(i,p),(j,s)\in\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}\setminus\{(i_{k},p_{k}),(i_{k+1},p_{k+1}),\ldots,(i_{r},p_{r})\}
, which proves our assertion.
∎

For
s\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}_{\leqslant s}\sqcup\{-\infty\}
, put

\Theta_{s}(\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm})\mathbin{:=}\mu_{\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}}({}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}),\quad\Omega_{s}(\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm})\mathbin{:=}\mu_{\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}}({}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\to}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}).

Lemma 9.18
.

We have

\Omega_{s}(\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm})\simeq\Theta_{s}\,\,\,\text{ and }\,\,\,\Theta_{s}(\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm})\simeq\Omega_{s}\quad\text{as valued quivers}.

Proof.

We only prove the second isomorphism for
\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}
since the proof of the other cases is almost identical.
In the proof of Lemma
9.17
,
we have seen that a mutation
\mu_{(i,p)}
for
(i,p)\in\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}

generates arrows between vertices in
\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{-}
and then they disappear in the course of the mutations
\mu_{(j,p^{\prime})}
’s for
(j,p^{\prime})\in\bm{\langle}{k,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}
located near
(i,p)
.
Moreover, the arrows adjacent to
(i,p)
are reversed during the mutations.
Hence we have
\Theta_{s}(\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+})\simeq\Omega_{s}
.
∎

Proposition 9.19
.

Every mutation
\mu_{(i,p)}
in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}}
on the cluster
\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}\}
corresponds to the quantum folded T-system in Theorem
6.9
.
Furthermore, each mutation
\mu_{(i,p)}
on
{}^{s}\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}
in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}}
corresponds to
\mathsf{T}_{\pm 2}
.

Proof.

For
(i,p)\neq(j,t)\in\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}
,
recall that the mutation
\mu_{(i,p)}
does not affect the arrows adjacent to
(j,s)
. Thus it suffices to consider
(i,p)
and vertices connected to
(i,p)
by arrows. Assume first that
(i,p)\in\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}
. Then by replacing vertices in
{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}
with
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{u}_{k,q}
’s, we have the following:

\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(k)}[a+1,b+1])}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b+2])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i,j},\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i,k},\mathsf{c}_{k,i}\lrcorner}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b])}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}[a+1,b+1])}

for
j,k
with
d(i,j),d(i,k)\leqslant 1

where
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b])
. Note that
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{u}_{k,q}
for
(k,q)\in\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{-}
never mutate by
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}}
. Hence the mutation rule for cluster variables can be expressed as

\displaystyle F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b])*\mu_{(i,p)}(F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b]))

\displaystyle=q^{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}(i,(b+2-a)/2)}F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b])\cdot F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b+2])

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+q^{\gamma(i,(b+2-a)/2)}\prod_{j;d(i,j)=1}F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}[a+1,b+1])^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}}.

Here
q^{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}(i,(b+2-a)/2)}
and
q^{\gamma(i,(b+2-a)/2)}
are computed by bar-invariance. Hence, as in Proposition
8.5
, and the above equation coincides
with the formula in Theorem
6.9
. Thus we have

\displaystyle\mu_{(i,p)}(F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b]))=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b+2]).

Thus the assertion for
\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}
follows.

Similarly, the arrows adjacent to
(i,p)
for
(i,p)\in\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{-}
can be depicted as follows:

\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(k)}[a-1,b-1])}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a-2,b])}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{k,i},\mathsf{c}_{i,k}\lrcorner}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b-2])}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}[a-1,b-1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{j,i},\mathsf{c}_{i,j}\lrcorner}

for
j,k
with
d(i,j),d(i,k)\leqslant 1
.

Then as in
\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}
, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\mu_{(i,p)}(F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b]))=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a-2,b-2]),

which proves our assertion.
∎

Example 9.20
.

By replacing vertices
(i,p)
in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,0}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}}({}^{0}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\leftarrow}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}})
with
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,0}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}}({{}^{0}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{u}_{i,p})

9.3

, we have the following
by Lemma
9.18
and Proposition

:

.

where the parameters of quantum cluster variables located at vertices that are vertically sink and horizontally source are shifted by
-2
, and the orientation of all arrows is reversed.

Thus we can conclude that

\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,0}\bm{\rangle}^{+}}(\mathfrak{S}_{0})\simeq\mathfrak{S}_{1}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,0}\bm{\rangle}^{-}}(\mathfrak{S}_{0})\simeq\mathfrak{S}_{-1}.

Following Example
9.20
,
it is straightforward to check the following proposition.

Proposition 9.21
.

For
s\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
, we have

\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}}(\mathfrak{S}_{s})\simeq\mathfrak{S}_{s\pm 1}.

For
s\in\mathbb{Z}\mspace{1mu}
, put

\mathfrak{U}\mathbin{:=}\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}}(\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}\}).

Proposition 9.22
.

Every mutation
\mu_{(i,p)}
in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}}
on the cluster
\mathfrak{U}

corresponds to the quantum folded T-system in Theorem
6.9
.
Furthermore, each mutation
\mu_{(i,p)}
on the quantum cluster variable at
(i,p)
in
\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}}
corresponds to
\mathsf{T}_{\mp 2}
.

Proof.

Set

\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{i,p}\}\mathbin{:=}\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}}(\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}\})\quad\text{ and }\quad\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{z}^{-}_{i,p}\}\mathbin{:=}\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{-}}(\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}\}).

In this proof, we only consider the case of
\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{z}^{+}_{i,p}\}
since the proof of
\{{}^{s}\mathfrak{z}^{-}_{i,p}\}
is parallel.
Let
(i,p)\in\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{\pm}
.

Case 1
.

(i,p)\in\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}
. By replacing vertices in
{}^{s}\hskip-1.72218pt\overset{\to}{\hbox to0.0pt{\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}\hss}\kern 0.39993pt\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.6pt\raisebox{6.49521pt}{\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}}\hss}\rule{6.00006pt}{0.4pt}\kern-0.39993pt\rotatebox{-30.0}{\rule[0.21529pt]{0.4pt}{7.70004pt}}}
with
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{z}_{k,q}^{+}
’s, we have the following:

\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(k)}[a+1,b+1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{k,i},\mathsf{c}_{i,k}\lrcorner}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b+2])}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b+2])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b])}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}[a+1,b+1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{j,i},\mathsf{c}_{i,j}\lrcorner}

for
j,k
with
d(i,j),d(i,k)\leqslant 1

where
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{z}_{i,p}^{+}=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b+2])
. Hence the mutation rule for quantum cluster variables can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mu_{(i,p)}(F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b+2]))*F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b+2])

\displaystyle=q^{{\mspace{1mu}\alpha}(i,(b+2-a)/2)}F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b])\cdot F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b+2])

\displaystyle\hskip-8.61108pt+q^{\gamma(i,(b+2-a)/2)}\prod_{j;d(i,j)=1}F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}[a+1,b+1])^{-\mathsf{c}_{j,i}}.

Thus we have

\mu_{(i,p)}(F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b+2]))=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b]).

Case 2
.

(i,p)\in\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{-}
. The arrows adjacent to
(i,p)
for
(i,p)\in\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{-}
are depicted as follows:

\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(k)}[a+1,b+1])}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b+2])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i,k},\mathsf{c}_{k,i}\lrcorner}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}[a+1,b+1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ulcorner-\mathsf{c}_{i,j},\mathsf{c}_{j,i}\lrcorner}

for
j,k
with
d(i,j),d(i,k)\leqslant 1

where
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{z}_{i,p}^{+}=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b])
.
Then as in
Case 1
, we have

\mu_{(i,p)}(F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a,b]))=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(i)}[a+2,b+2]).\qed

Now, we are ready to prove Proposition
9.8
.

Proof of Proposition
9.8
.

Let us define

\displaystyle\bm{\mu}_{\bm{+}}\mathbin{:=}\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s+1}\bm{\rangle}^{-}}\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{+}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\bm{\mu}_{\bm{-}}\mathbin{:=}\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s-1}\bm{\rangle}^{+}}\circ\mu_{\bm{\langle}{-\infty,s}\bm{\rangle}^{-}}.

It follows from Propositions
9.19
,
9.21
and
9.22
that

\displaystyle\bm{\mu}_{\bm{+}}(\mathfrak{S}_{s})\simeq\mathfrak{S}_{s+2}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\bm{\mu}_{\bm{-}}(\mathfrak{S}_{s})\simeq\mathfrak{S}_{s-2}.

By applying
\bm{\mu}_{\bm{+}}
repeatedly,
we obtain
F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{i,p})
for
(i,p)\in{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
with
p\geqslant s
as a cluster variable of
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathscr{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
.
Similarly, we obtain every
F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{i,p})
for
(i,p)\in{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
with
p\leqslant s
as a cluster variable of
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathscr{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
by using the repetition of
\bm{\mu}_{\bm{-}}
.
Thus the cluster algebra
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathscr{A}_{q}(\mathfrak{g})
contains every
F_{q}(\mathsf{X}_{i,p})
associated to
{\widetilde{{\triangle\hskip-5.89856pt{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\hskip 3.69885pt}}}_{0}
as its cluster variables.
∎

Conjecture 5
.

Let
s
be an arbitrary integer.
If
F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}[a,b])\in\mathfrak{K}_{q}(\mathsf{g})

q
-commutes with
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}
for all
{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{u}_{i,p}\in\mathfrak{S}_{s}
, then there exists
(j,l)\in{\widetilde{\mathop{\mathbin{\mbox{\large$\blacktriangle$}}}\hskip-8.61108pt\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{\scalebox{.53}{ {\color[rgb]{1,1,1} ${\mathbf{f}}$}}}}\ }}_{0}
such that

{{}^{s}}\hskip-0.86108pt\mathfrak{u}_{j,l}=F_{q}(\underline{m}^{(j)}[a,b]).
